If the Rook moves to d3, it's not a check. White captures it: Kxd3.
If the Rook moves to d4, it's not a check.
If the Rook moves to d5, it's not a check.
If the Rook moves to d6, it's not a check.

b3b4: Attacks nothing, just moves a pawn.
f2f3: As said, Bishop at c6 can capture f3. (c6 to f3: delta-col=3, delta-row=-3).
h2h3 or h2h4: Simple pawn moves.

Comparing c3c2 and c3b2.
c3b2: King moves to b2. Now King is at b2.
Rook at d1 is not attacked.
Does and can the Rook check the King at b2?
Rook at d1: attack d1-d8 and a1-h1.
King at b2 is on rank 2. Rook at d1 is on rank 1.
Rook doesn't check b2.
But the Rook can move to b1, giving check! (rd1-b1+).
If White plays c3b2, and Black plays rd1-b1+, the King must move.
If White plays c3c2, and Black plays rd1-d2+, White captures the rook.

So c3c2 is much stronger.
